<?php

namespace App\Models;

use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Model;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Relations\BelongsTo;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Relations\HasMany;

class CaseRecord extends Model
{
    // Case fields allowed for mass assignment after the admin-only controller validates the request.
    protected $fillable = [
        'student_id',
        'created_by',
        'appointment_id',
        'case_number',
        'title',
        'category',
        'description',
        'remarks',
        'session_date',
        'session_time',
        'session_end_time',
        'findings',
        'recommendations',
        'status',
    ];

    public function student(): BelongsTo
    {
        // Student whose guidance history this private record describes.
        return $this->belongsTo(User::class, 'student_id');
    }

    public function creator(): BelongsTo
    {
        // Admin who opened the case; useful for identifying the original record owner.
        return $this->belongsTo(User::class, 'created_by');
    }

    public function appointment(): BelongsTo
    {
        // Optional consultation linked to this case, if the case was opened from a request.
        return $this->belongsTo(Appointment::class);
    }

    public function followUps(): HasMany
    {
        // One case may have many dated notes; show the newest follow-up first on case detail pages.
        return $this->hasMany(FollowUp::class)->orderByDesc('follow_up_date');
    }
}

<?php

namespace App\Models;

use App\Models\Appointment;
use Database\Factories\UserFactory;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Attributes\Fillable;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Attributes\Hidden;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Factories\HasFactory;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Relations\HasMany;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Relations\BelongsTo;
use Illuminate\Foundation\Auth\User as Authenticatable;
use Illuminate\Notifications\Notifiable;

#[Fillable([
    'name', 'email', 'password', 'role', 'assigned_teacher_id', 'student_number', 'grade_level', 'section',
    'contact_number', 'address', 'guardian_name', 'guardian_relationship', 'guardian_contact_number',
])]
#[Hidden(['password', 'remember_token'])]
class User extends Authenticatable
{
    /** @use HasFactory<UserFactory> */
    use HasFactory, Notifiable;

    protected function casts(): array
    {
        // Laravel hashes a password before it is saved to the database.
        return [
            'email_verified_at' => 'datetime',
            'password' => 'hashed',
        ];
    }

    public function appointmentsAsStudent(): HasMany
    {
        // Appointments where this user is the student receiving guidance.
        return $this->hasMany(Appointment::class, 'student_id');
    }

    public function assignedTeacher(): BelongsTo
    {
        // Teacher responsible for this student; null for accounts without an assignment.
        return $this->belongsTo(self::class, 'assigned_teacher_id');
    }

    public function assignedStudents(): HasMany
    {
        // Students linked to this teacher account.
        return $this->hasMany(self::class, 'assigned_teacher_id')->where('role', 'student');
    }

    public function appointmentsAssigned(): HasMany
    {
        // Consultations where this account is the assigned guidance administrator.
        return $this->hasMany(Appointment::class, 'assigned_to');
    }

    public function createdCases(): HasMany
    {
        // Case records authored by this account; used to preserve history on account deletion.
        return $this->hasMany(CaseRecord::class, 'created_by');
    }
}

FILE: app/Providers/FortifyServiceProvider.php
SIZE: 1920 bytes
==============================================================================
<?php

namespace App\Providers;

use App\Actions\Fortify\CreateNewUser;
use App\Actions\Fortify\ResetUserPassword;
use App\Actions\Fortify\UpdateUserPassword;
use App\Actions\Fortify\UpdateUserProfileInformation;
use Illuminate\Cache\RateLimiting\Limit;
use Illuminate\Http\Request;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\RateLimiter;
use Illuminate\Support\ServiceProvider;
use Illuminate\Support\Str;
use Laravel\Fortify\Actions\RedirectIfTwoFactorAuthenticatable;
use Laravel\Fortify\Fortify;

class FortifyServiceProvider extends ServiceProvider
{
    /**
     * Register any application services.
     */
    public function register(): void
    {
        //
    }

    /**
     * Bootstrap any application services.
     */
    public function boot(): void
    {   
        Fortify::loginView(fn () => view('auth.login'));
        Fortify::createUsersUsing(CreateNewUser::class);
        Fortify::updateUserProfileInformationUsing(UpdateUserProfileInformation::class);
        Fortify::updateUserPasswordsUsing(UpdateUserPassword::class);
        Fortify::resetUserPasswordsUsing(ResetUserPassword::class);
        Fortify::redirectUserForTwoFactorAuthenticationUsing(RedirectIfTwoFactorAuthenticatable::class);

        RateLimiter::for('login', function (Request $request) {
            $throttleKey = Str::transliterate(Str::lower($request->input(Fortify::username())).'|'.$request->ip());

            return Limit::perMinute(5)->by($throttleKey);
        });

        RateLimiter::for('two-factor', function (Request $request) {
            return Limit::perMinute(5)->by($request->session()->get('login.id'));
        });

        RateLimiter::for('passkeys', function (Request $request) {
            $credentialId = $request->input('credential.id');

            return Limit::perMinute(10)->by(
                ($credentialId ?: $request->session()->getId()).'|'.$request->ip()
            );
        });
    }
}

<?php

use App\Http\Middleware\AppendMis215EggHeader;
use Illuminate\Foundation\Application;
use Illuminate\Foundation\Configuration\Exceptions;
use Illuminate\Foundation\Configuration\Middleware;
use Illuminate\Http\Request;

return Application::configure(basePath: dirname(__DIR__))
    ->withRouting(
        web: __DIR__.'/../routes/web.php',
        api: __DIR__.'/../routes/api.php',
        commands: __DIR__.'/../routes/console.php',
        health: '/up',
    )
    ->withMiddleware(function (Middleware $middleware): void {
        $middleware->web(append: [
            AppendMis215EggHeader::class,
        ]);
    })
    ->withExceptions(function (Exceptions $exceptions): void {
        $exceptions->shouldRenderJsonWhen(
            fn (Request $request) => $request->is('api/*') || $request->expectsJson(),
        );
    })->create();

return [

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | Default Database Connection Name
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------

use Illuminate\Support\Str;

return [

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | Default Session Driver
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    |
    | This option determines the default session driver that is utilized for
    | incoming requests. Laravel supports a variety of storage options to
    | persist session data. Database storage is a great default choice.
    |
    | Supported: "file", "cookie", "database", "memcached",
    |            "redis", "dynamodb", "array"
    |
    */

    'driver' => env('SESSION_DRIVER', 'database'),

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | Session Lifetime
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    |
    | Here you may specify the number of minutes that you wish the session
    | to be allowed to remain idle before it expires. If you want them
    | to expire immediately when the browser is closed then you may
    | indicate that via the expire_on_close configuration option.
    |
    */

    'lifetime' => (int) env('SESSION_LIFETIME', 120),

    'expire_on_close' => env('SESSION_EXPIRE_ON_CLOSE', false),

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | Session Encryption
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    |
    | This option allows you to easily specify that all of your session data
    | should be encrypted before it's stored. All encryption is performed
    | automatically by Laravel and you may use the session like normal.
    |
    */

    'encrypt' => env('SESSION_ENCRYPT', false),

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | Session File Location
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    |
    | When utilizing the "file" session driver, the session files are placed
    | on disk. The default storage location is defined here; however, you
    | are free to provide another location where they should be stored.
    |
    */

    'files' => storage_path('framework/sessions'),

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | Session Database Connection
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    |
    | When using the "database" or "redis" session drivers, you may specify a
    | connection that should be used to manage these sessions. This should
    | correspond to a connection in your database configuration options.
    |
    */

    'connection' => env('SESSION_CONNECTION'),

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | Session Database Table
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    |
    | When using the "database" session driver, you may specify the table to
    | be used to store sessions. Of course, a sensible default is defined
    | for you; however, you're welcome to change this to another table.
    |
    */

    'table' => env('SESSION_TABLE', 'sessions'),

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | Session Cache Store
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    |
    | When using one of the framework's cache driven session backends, you may
    | define the cache store which should be used to store the session data
    | between requests. This must match one of your defined cache stores.
    |
    | Affects: "dynamodb", "memcached", "redis"
    |
    */

    'store' => env('SESSION_STORE'),

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | Session Sweeping Lottery
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    |
    | Some session drivers must manually sweep their storage location to get
    | rid of old sessions from storage. Here are the chances that it will
    | happen on a given request. By default, the odds are 2 out of 100.
    |
    */

    'lottery' => [2, 100],

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | Session Cookie Name
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    |
    | Here you may change the name of the session cookie that is created by
    | the framework. Typically, you should not need to change this value
    | since doing so does not grant a meaningful security improvement.
    |
    */

    'cookie' => env(
        'SESSION_COOKIE',
        Str::slug((string) env('APP_NAME', 'laravel')).'-session'
    ),

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | Session Cookie Path
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    |
    | The session cookie path determines the path for which the cookie will
    | be regarded as available. Typically, this will be the root path of
    | your application, but you're free to change this when necessary.
    |
    */

    'path' => env('SESSION_PATH', '/'),

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | Session Cookie Domain
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    |
    | This value determines the domain and subdomains the session cookie is
    | available to. By default, the cookie will be available to the root
    | domain without subdomains. Typically, this shouldn't be changed.
    |
    */

    'domain' => env('SESSION_DOMAIN'),

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | HTTPS Only Cookies
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    |
    | By setting this option to true, session cookies will only be sent back
    | to the server if the browser has a HTTPS connection. This will keep
    | the cookie from being sent to you when it can't be done securely.
    |
    */

    'secure' => env('SESSION_SECURE_COOKIE'),

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | HTTP Access Only
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    |
    | Setting this value to true will prevent JavaScript from accessing the
    | value of the cookie and the cookie will only be accessible through
    | the HTTP protocol. It's unlikely you should disable this option.
    |
    */

    'http_only' => env('SESSION_HTTP_ONLY', true),

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | Same-Site Cookies
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    |
    | This option determines how your cookies behave when cross-site requests
    | take place, and can be used to mitigate CSRF attacks. By default, we
    | will set this value to "lax" to permit secure cross-site requests.
    |
    | See: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Headers/Set-Cookie#samesitesamesite-value
    |
    | Supported: "lax", "strict", "none", null
    |
    */

    'same_site' => env('SESSION_SAME_SITE', 'lax'),

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | Partitioned Cookies
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    |
    | Setting this value to true will tie the cookie to the top-level site for
    | a cross-site context. Partitioned cookies are accepted by the browser
    | when flagged "secure" and the Same-Site attribute is set to "none".
    |
    */

    'partitioned' => env('SESSION_PARTITIONED_COOKIE', false),

    /*
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    | Session Serialization
    |--------------------------------------------------------------------------
    |
    | This value controls the serialization strategy for session data, which
    | is JSON by default. Setting this to "php" allows the storage of PHP
    | objects in the session but can make an application vulnerable to
    | "gadget chain" serialization attacks if the APP_KEY is leaked.
    |
    | Supported: "json", "php"
    |
    */

    'serialization' => 'json',

];

FILE: database/factories/UserFactory.php

<?php

namespace Database\Factories;

use App\Models\User;
use Illuminate\Database\Eloquent\Factories\Factory;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Hash;
use Illuminate\Support\Str;

/**
 * @extends Factory<User>
 */
class UserFactory extends Factory
{
    /**
     * The current password being used by the factory.
     */
    protected static ?string $password;

    /**
     * Define the model's default state.
     *
     * @return array<string, mixed>
     */
    public function definition(): array
    {
        return [
            'name' => fake()->name(),
            'email' => fake()->unique()->safeEmail(),
            'email_verified_at' => now(),
            'password' => static::$password ??= Hash::make('password'),
            'remember_token' => Str::random(10),
        ];
    }

    /**
     * Indicate that the model's email address should be unverified.
     */
    public function unverified(): static
    {
        return $this->state(fn (array $attributes) => [
            'email_verified_at' => null,
        ]);
    }
}

use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Schema;

{
    public function up(): void
    {
        Schema::create('appointments', function (Blueprint $table) {
            // A consultation request connects a student, the creator, and optionally assigned guidance staff.
            $table->id();
            // Deleting a student removes their requests; deleting staff preserves requests but clears staff references.
            $table->foreignId('student_id')->constrained('users')->cascadeOnDelete();
            $table->foreignId('created_by')->nullable()->constrained('users')->nullOnDelete();
            $table->foreignId('assigned_to')->nullable()->constrained('users')->nullOnDelete();
            $table->string('title');
            $table->text('reason')->nullable();
            // Null means staff have received the request but have not assigned a consultation time yet.
            $table->dateTime('scheduled_at')->nullable();
            $table->integer('duration_minutes')->default(30);
            $table->string('status')->default('pending');
            $table->text('notes')->nullable(); // Private staff notes; deliberately omitted from student/API projections.
            $table->timestamps();
            // Indexes make common student, status, and date searches faster.
            $table->index(['student_id', 'status']);
            $table->index(['status', 'scheduled_at']);
            $table->index('created_by');
            $table->index('assigned_to');
        });
    }

    public function down(): void
    {
        Schema::dropIfExists('appointments');

        Schema::create('passkeys', function (Blueprint $table) {
            $table->id();
            // A passkey belongs to one account and is removed if that account is deleted.
            $table->foreignIdFor(Passkeys::userModel(), 'user_id')->constrained()->cascadeOnDelete();
            $table->string('name');
            $table->string('credential_id')->unique(); // Credential IDs must not be registered to two accounts.
            $table->json('credential'); // Stores the package-managed public-key credential data.

<?php

use Illuminate\Database\Migrations\Migration;
use Illuminate\Database\Schema\Blueprint;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Schema;

return new class extends Migration
{
    public function up(): void
    {
        Schema::create('case_records', function (Blueprint $table) {
            // Confidential guidance case linked to a student and optionally an appointment.
            $table->id();
            // Student deletion removes their private case; creator deletion is restricted to preserve authorship.
            $table->foreignId('student_id')->constrained('users')->cascadeOnDelete();
            $table->foreignId('created_by')->constrained('users')->restrictOnDelete();
            // A case can exist without an appointment; removing a linked appointment keeps the case itself.
            $table->foreignId('appointment_id')->nullable()->constrained('appointments')->nullOnDelete();
            $table->string('case_number')->unique(); // Unique staff reference number.
            $table->string('title');
            $table->text('description')->nullable();
            $table->string('status')->default('open');
            $table->string('category')->default('other');
            $table->text('remarks')->nullable();
            $table->date('session_date')->nullable();
            $table->time('session_time')->nullable();
            $table->time('session_end_time')->nullable();
            $table->text('findings')->nullable();
            $table->text('recommendations')->nullable();
            $table->timestamps();
            // Indexes support the case list filters.
            $table->index(['status', 'category']);
            $table->index('student_id');
            $table->index('created_by');
            $table->index('appointment_id');
        });
    }

    public function down(): void

use Illuminate\Database\Migrations\Migration;
use Illuminate\Database\Schema\Blueprint;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Schema;

return new class extends Migration
{
    public function up(): void
    {
        Schema::create('follow_ups', function (Blueprint $table) {
            // Dated notes and actions recorded after a guidance case is created.
            $table->id();
            // Follow-ups are part of the case history and are removed with their parent case.
            $table->foreignId('case_record_id')->constrained('case_records')->cascadeOnDelete();
            // Retain the author account while notes depend on it so historical attribution is not lost.
            $table->foreignId('created_by')->constrained('users')->restrictOnDelete();
            $table->date('follow_up_date');
            $table->text('notes');
            $table->string('action_taken')->nullable();
            $table->timestamps();
            $table->index('created_by');
            $table->index(['case_record_id', 'follow_up_date']);
        });
    }

    public function down(): void
    {
        Schema::dropIfExists('follow_ups');
    }

<?php

namespace Database\Seeders;

use App\Models\User;
use App\Models\Appointment;
use App\Models\CaseRecord;
use App\Models\FollowUp;
use Illuminate\Database\Console\Seeds\WithoutModelEvents;
use Illuminate\Database\Seeder;

class DatabaseSeeder extends Seeder
{
    use WithoutModelEvents;

    /**
     * Seed the application's database.
     */
    public function run(): void
    {
        // These accounts and linked records are fictional local-demo fixtures, not production accounts.
        // firstOrCreate makes the default seed safe to rerun without duplicating the same demo rows.
        $admin = User::firstOrCreate(['email' => 'admin@guidancesched.test'], [
            'name' => 'Demo Guidance Admin', 'role' => 'admin', 'password' => 'ChangeMe123!',
        ]);
        $teacher = User::firstOrCreate(['email' => 'teacher@guidancesched.test'], [
            'name' => 'Demo Teacher', 'role' => 'teacher', 'password' => 'ChangeMe123!',
        ]);
        $student = User::firstOrCreate(['email' => 'student@guidancesched.test'], [
            'name' => 'Demo Student', 'role' => 'student', 'password' => 'ChangeMe123!',
        ]);

        // Build one small, connected example so local users can explore the appointment-to-case workflow.
        $appointment = Appointment::firstOrCreate(
            ['student_id' => $student->id, 'title' => 'Study planning consultation'],
            ['created_by' => $admin->id, 'reason' => 'Discussing study routines (fictional demo data).', 'scheduled_at' => now()->addDays(2), 'status' => 'pending'],
        );

        $case = CaseRecord::firstOrCreate(
            ['case_number' => 'DEMO-CASE-001'],
            ['student_id' => $student->id, 'created_by' => $admin->id, 'appointment_id' => $appointment->id,
                'title' => 'Study support', 'category' => 'academic', 'description' => 'Synthetic demonstration case.', 'status' => 'open'],
        );

        FollowUp::firstOrCreate(
            ['case_record_id' => $case->id, 'follow_up_date' => today()->toDateString()],
            ['created_by' => $admin->id, 'notes' => 'Synthetic follow-up entry for demonstration.', 'action_taken' => 'Discussed study plan'],
        );
    }
}

FILE: database/seeders/LoadTestDataSeeder.php
SIZE: 7172 bytes
==============================================================================
<?php

namespace Database\Seeders;

use App\Models\Appointment;
use App\Models\CaseRecord;
use App\Models\FollowUp;
use App\Models\User;
use Illuminate\Database\Seeder;
use Illuminate\Support\Carbon;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Hash;
use InvalidArgumentException;
use RuntimeException;

class LoadTestDataSeeder extends Seeder
{
    public function run(): void
    {
        // Avoid accidentally inserting a large synthetic dataset into a live database.
        if (app()->environment('production') && ! filter_var(env('LOAD_TEST_ALLOW_PRODUCTION', false), FILTER_VALIDATE_BOOLEAN)) {
            throw new RuntimeException('Load-test seeding is disabled in production. Set LOAD_TEST_ALLOW_PRODUCTION=true only for a controlled test.');
        }

        // Counts can be increased in the local .env to match the scale of a planned load test.
        $teacherCount = $this->positiveCount('LOAD_TEST_TEACHERS', 10);
        $studentCount = $this->positiveCount('LOAD_TEST_STUDENTS', 250);
        $appointmentCount = $this->positiveCount('LOAD_TEST_APPOINTMENTS', 2000);
        $caseCount = $this->positiveCount('LOAD_TEST_CASES', 500);
        $followUpsPerCase = $this->positiveCount('LOAD_TEST_FOLLOW_UPS_PER_CASE', 2);

        $admin = User::query()->where('role', 'admin')->firstOrFail();
        $password = Hash::make((string) env('LOAD_TEST_PASSWORD', 'LoadTestPass123!'));

        // Stable email keys let reruns update the same test accounts instead of creating duplicates.
        $teachers = collect(range(1, $teacherCount))->map(function (int $number) use ($password): User {
            return User::query()->updateOrCreate(
                ['email' => sprintf('loadtest.teacher.%04d@guidancesched.test', $number)],
                [
                    'name' => sprintf('Load Test Teacher %04d', $number),
                    'role' => 'teacher',
                    'password' => $password,
                ],
            );
        })->values();

        $students = collect(range(1, $studentCount))->map(function (int $number) use ($teachers, $password): User {
            $teacher = $teachers[($number - 1) % $teachers->count()];

            return User::query()->updateOrCreate(
                ['email' => sprintf('loadtest.student.%05d@guidancesched.test', $number)],
                [
                    'name' => sprintf('Load Test Student %05d', $number),
                    'role' => 'student',
                    'password' => $password,
                    'student_number' => sprintf('LOAD-%06d', $number),
                    'grade_level' => sprintf('Grade %d', (($number - 1) % 6) + 7),
                    'section' => sprintf('Synthetic Section %02d', (($number - 1) % 12) + 1),
                    'assigned_teacher_id' => $teacher->id,
                ],
            );
        })->values();

        // Distribute requests across the generated students and teachers, cycling through every status.
        $appointments = collect(range(1, $appointmentCount))->map(function (int $number) use ($admin, $students, $teachers): Appointment {
            $student = $students[($number - 1) % $students->count()];
            $teacher = $teachers[($number - 1) % $teachers->count()];
            $status = Appointment::STATUSES[($number - 1) % count(Appointment::STATUSES)];
            $scheduledAt = in_array($status, ['pending', 'approved'], true)
                ? now()->addDays(($number % 60) + 1)->setTime(8 + ($number % 8), ($number % 2) * 30)
                : now()->subDays(($number % 180) + 1)->setTime(8 + ($number % 8), ($number % 2) * 30);

            return Appointment::query()->updateOrCreate(
                [
                    'student_id' => $student->id,
                    'title' => sprintf('Load-test consultation %06d', $number),
                ],
                [
                    'created_by' => $teacher->id,
                    'assigned_to' => $admin->id,
                    'reason' => 'Synthetic load-test reason. No real person or case is represented.',
                    'scheduled_at' => $scheduledAt,
                    'duration_minutes' => 30,
                    'status' => $status,
                    'notes' => 'Synthetic load-test note.',
                ],
            );
        })->values();

        // Give generated cases links to generated students and appointments for realistic list/detail queries.
        $cases = collect(range(1, $caseCount))->map(function (int $number) use ($admin, $students, $appointments): CaseRecord {
            $studentIndex = ($number - 1) % $students->count();
            $appointment = $appointments[($number - 1) % $appointments->count()];

            return CaseRecord::query()->updateOrCreate(
                ['case_number' => sprintf('LOADTEST-%06d', $number)],
                [
                    'student_id' => $students[$studentIndex]->id,
                    'created_by' => $admin->id,
                    'appointment_id' => $appointment->id,
                    'title' => sprintf('Synthetic support case %06d', $number),
                    'description' => 'Synthetic load-test description. No real person or confidential information is represented.',
                    'status' => ['open', 'in_progress', 'closed'][($number - 1) % 3],
                    'category' => ['academic', 'behavioral', 'personal'][($number - 1) % 3],
                ],
            );
        })->values();

        // Anchor follow-up dates to the case creation date so rerunning the seeder keeps the same keys.
        foreach ($cases as $caseIndex => $case) {
            for ($followUpNumber = 1; $followUpNumber <= $followUpsPerCase; $followUpNumber++) {
                $date = Carbon::parse($case->created_at)->subDays($followUpNumber * 7 + ($caseIndex % 30))->toDateString();

                FollowUp::query()->updateOrCreate(
                    [
                        'case_record_id' => $case->id,
                        'follow_up_date' => $date,
                    ],
                    [
                        'created_by' => $case->created_by,
                        'notes' => 'Synthetic load-test follow-up. No real person or confidential information is represented.',
                        'action_taken' => 'Recorded synthetic follow-up',
                    ],
                );
            }
        }

        $this->command?->info(sprintf(
            'Synthetic load-test data ready: %d teachers, %d students, %d appointments, %d cases, and up to %d follow-ups.',
            $teachers->count(),
            $students->count(),
            $appointments->count(),
            $cases->count(),
            $cases->count() * $followUpsPerCase,
        ));
    }

    private function positiveCount(string $environmentVariable, int $default): int
    {
        // A zero/negative count could leave later records without teachers, students, or parent rows.
        $count = (int) env($environmentVariable, $default);

        if ($count < 1) {
            throw new InvalidArgumentException(sprintf('%s must be a positive integer.', $environmentVariable));
        }

        return $count;
    }
}

# GuidanceSched: presentation outline

Use this outline to explain the system in 11 slides. It is based on the project files. Any proposed improvements are labeled as recommendations. No load-test results are available yet.

## 1. Title

**GuidanceSched**  
A school guidance appointment and case-record system  
How it works, how it is set up, and how it protects information

Presenter: [Name] · School: [School] · Date: [Date]

## 2. Organization and problem

- **Organization:** a school guidance center that arranges student consultations and follow-up.
- **Problem:** appointment requests, student details, private case records, and follow-up notes can be hard to keep organized when tracked separately.
- **What the system does:** helps staff manage appointments, accounts, case records, and follow-ups. It also provides different pages for different users and a few ways for other programs to exchange data with it.
- **Project limit:** this is a school project prototype. It has not had a formal school privacy review and is not ready to be described as a production counseling system.

## 3. Users and main tasks

| User | What they can do |
| --- | --- |
| Admin / guidance staff | Manage accounts; schedule, approve, complete, or cancel appointments; view and update case records; add follow-up notes |
| Teacher | Work with assigned students; create consultation requests and set available times; view related appointments |
| Student | View their own assigned consultation date, time, and status; staff create requests for them |

Main tasks:

1. A staff member creates an appointment request for a student.
2. A teacher sets a time, or an admin schedules and approves it.
3. Staff records a case and can add follow-up notes later.
4. An admin manages user accounts.

## 4. Three main parts of the system

```mermaid
flowchart TB
  subgraph P[Part 1: What users see]
    B[Web pages in a browser]
    C[Other programs using the API]
  end
  subgraph A[Part 2: What processes requests]
    R[Laravel routes and login checks]
    CT[Controllers: check and handle requests]
    M[Models: work with the system's data]
  end
  subgraph D[Part 3: Where information is stored]
    PG[(PostgreSQL database)]
  end
  B -->|Web requests and pages| R
  C -->|Requests and JSON data| R
  R --> CT --> M --> PG
```

- **Part 1 — What users see:** web pages in a browser. Other programs can also use the system through its API, a few web addresses for exchanging information.
- **Part 2 — What processes requests:** the Laravel app checks who is signed in, checks what they are allowed to do, and handles their request.
- **Part 3 — Where information is stored:** the PostgreSQL database saves users, appointments, case records, and follow-ups.
- These are three parts of how the system works. They are not three separate apps: the current setup runs one Laravel app and one database.

## 5. How a teacher schedules an appointment

Example: a teacher sends an appointment request with a chosen time.

1. The teacher submits a form on a web page or sends a request through the API.
2. The Laravel app receives the request.
3. It checks that the teacher is signed in and allowed to work with that student.
4. It checks that the student and time are valid and that the time is available.
5. The app saves the appointment as **pending**, with the student, teacher, and time. The default appointment length is 30 minutes.
6. The database saves the appointment. The system then shows an updated page or sends back a result to the API user.
7. An admin can later approve, complete, or cancel the appointment.

The system can reject a request if the user is not signed in (API: 401), is not allowed to do it (403), or entered invalid information (422).

## 6. How parts of the system communicate

| Connection | What happens |
| --- | --- |
| Browser to app | The browser sends a web request and receives a page. Users sign in to the website. Use HTTPS, the secure form of web traffic, when people connect over a network. |
| Other program to app | A program can use the API to send or receive information as JSON, a simple text format computers use to share data. It signs in with an existing account. Use HTTPS when sending login details over a network. |
| App to database | The app sends database requests over the private Docker network. Database login details come from settings outside the source code. |

- The API shows lists in smaller pages of results. It returns JSON data and uses error codes when something goes wrong.
- Students see only limited information about their own appointments. Only admins can use the case and follow-up API addresses.
- The API uses Basic Authentication, a simple sign-in method. Before real use, the school should review whether a stronger sign-in method is needed.

## 7. Where the system runs

**Current setup for development and demos:** Docker Compose starts the app and database.

- **App container:** runs the GuidanceSched website using PHP 8.5.
- **Database container:** runs PostgreSQL 18 and stores the system's information.
- Docker connects the two containers on a private network. The database port is only opened to the local computer in the checked-in setup.
- The app's project files are shared with its container so developers can work on them.

**Recommendation before shared use:** put the app behind a secure HTTPS connection, keep the database private, protect passwords and other secret settings, and set up monitored backups. The project does not include a complete setup for a live production service.

## 8. What information is stored

| Record | Examples of stored information |
| --- | --- |
| Users | Name, email, password saved in protected form, role, student and guardian contact details, assigned teacher |
| Appointments | Student, staff member, title, reason, date and time, length, status, staff-only notes |
| Cases | Student, staff member, case number, title, description, category, status, session details, findings and recommendations |
| Follow-ups | Case, staff member, date, notes, action taken |
| Web sessions | Information that keeps a user signed in while using the website |

- The database connects related records, such as a case to a student or an appointment to its creator.
- Case descriptions, findings, recommendations, and follow-up notes should be restricted to authorized staff. The appointment API leaves out staff-only notes.
- Database backups should be kept somewhere protected. Test restoring a backup only in a separate test database.
- The school should decide how long records are kept and when they are deleted. The project does not set these rules.

## 9. Security and student privacy (RA 10173)

- RA 10173, the Data Privacy Act of 2012, says personal information must be handled with transparency, for a proper purpose, and only as much as needed. See the [National Privacy Commission's copy of the law](https://privacy.gov.ph/data-privacy-act/).
- **Already in the project:** users must sign in; the app checks user roles and access; only admins can view case and follow-up records; forms check the information entered; passwords are saved in protected form; students see limited appointment details; anyone cannot sign themselves up.
- **Private information in the system:** student and guardian details, appointment reasons, case descriptions, findings, recommendations, and follow-up notes.
- **Before using real student information:** the school should explain why it collects information, who can see it, how it is protected, and when it is deleted. Use secure connections, protect backups, review staff access, and plan what to do if information is exposed.
- These system features do not prove that the school follows the law. Ask the school's Data Protection Officer to review the system before using real student records. Use made-up records for demos.

## 10. Checking speed and comparing designs

**Speed test status:** the project has no recorded test results. Run the test with made-up data before presenting any speed numbers.

When testing, record:

- Date, PHP and database versions, and computer or server used.
- Which page or API address was tested, how many requests were sent, and how many at once.
- How many requests succeeded or failed, how long they took, and how many requests finished per second.
- Repeat the test and note whether the app had already been used recently.

Suggested small test: request the appointment list 100 times, with 5 requests at once, using the made-up admin account. This is only a basic check, not proof the system can handle a large school. Do not test with real student information or on a shared live system without approval.

| Design | Possible benefit | Possible cost |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Current design: one Laravel app and one database | Easier to set up and maintain for a small project; related changes can be saved together | If more capacity is needed, the app is scaled as a whole; all features share one release |
| Split the system into several apps | Each app could be changed or scaled separately | More pieces to set up, connect, secure, and monitor; keeping information in sync is harder |

To compare the designs, test the same tasks on the same computer or server. Compare speed, errors, computer use, ease of maintenance, and whether information stays consistent. No such comparison has been run yet; the table lists expected trade-offs only.

## 11. Limits and next steps

**Current limits**

- This is a prototype and has not had a formal school privacy review.
- No speed test or design comparison results have been recorded.
- The API sign-in and local Docker setup need review before shared or real use.
- The school has not set record-keeping time limits or a full plan for monitoring and responding to problems.
- The local setup does not include a complete plan for keeping a live service available or recovering from a major failure.

**Next steps**

1. Ask school staff and the Data Protection Officer to review the work process, information collected, who can see it, and when it should be deleted.
2. Review sign-in, secure connections, passwords and secret settings, activity records, and how access is checked.
3. Write down how to back up and restore information and respond to a security problem.
4. Run and record speed tests using made-up records.
5. Try the system with made-up records first. Use real student information only after school approval.

---

**Project files used:** `README.md`, `compose.yaml`, web and API routes, appointment controllers, and database setup files. Laravel Boost setup could not finish because Composer could not connect to Packagist; this outline was prepared by reading the project files directly.

# How GuidanceSched was built and how to try it

This guide explains the project in plain language, using WSL Ubuntu, Docker Desktop, PostgreSQL, Laravel, and Render.

## 1. What each tool does

- **WSL + Ubuntu:** your Linux workspace inside Windows. Keep the project in Ubuntu's files, such as `~/projects/GuidanceSched`, instead of under `/mnt/c` for better Docker performance.
- **Docker Desktop:** runs containers, which are separate boxes for software.
- **Docker Compose:** starts the app box and database box together and connects them.
- **Laravel:** the PHP app that shows web pages, checks sign-ins, applies rules, and saves information.
- **PostgreSQL:** the database that stores accounts, appointments, cases, and follow-ups.
- **Render:** builds the Docker image from GitHub and runs it online with a separate PostgreSQL database.

## 2. How this website was put together

1. **Define the users and tasks.** The project has admin/guidance staff, teachers, and students, each with different access.
2. **Create the Laravel app.** Laravel handles web pages, sign-in, form checks, and the rules for who can view or change records.
3. **Create the database tables.** Migrations describe the PostgreSQL tables for users, appointments, cases, follow-ups, and web sessions.
4. **Connect pages and data.** Routes send each browser or API request to a controller. Controllers check the user and request, then Laravel models read or update PostgreSQL.
5. **Add the web pages and API.** Blade is Laravel's tool for making pages shown in a browser. The API is a small set of web addresses other programs can use to exchange data in JSON, a simple text format.
6. **Put the app and database in Docker.** `compose.yaml` describes the local app and PostgreSQL containers. `Dockerfile` describes how to build the app container for local or online use.
7. **Add made-up demo records.** The local database seeder creates fictional accounts and example data. The online container does not run the seeder automatically, so it cannot publish the repository's known demo passwords.

## 3. Prepare WSL Ubuntu and Docker Desktop

1. Install Docker Desktop for Windows and enable **Settings → Resources → WSL Integration** for your Ubuntu distribution.
2. Open Ubuntu and check Docker is available:

   ```bash
   docker --version
   docker compose version
   ```

3. Install Git, PHP **8.4.1 or later**, Composer, and the PHP extensions Composer requests. This project cannot run with PHP 8.1. Check versions:

   ```bash
   php -v
   composer -V
   ```

4. Put the project in Ubuntu's Linux home directory, for example `~/projects/GuidanceSched`, then open that folder in the Ubuntu terminal.
5. Make sure the repository files are available there. If cloning for the first time:

   ```bash
   mkdir -p ~/projects
   cd ~/projects
   git clone https://github.com/duranafloramaesanchez/GuidanceSched.git
   cd GuidanceSched
   ```

## 4. Start the website locally

Run these from the project folder in Ubuntu:

1. Install the PHP packages first. This creates the Sail files that the Compose setup uses:

   ```bash
   composer install
   ```

2. Create your local settings file:

   ```bash
   cp .env.example .env
   ```

   In `.env`, keep `DB_CONNECTION=pgsql` and set the local values to match Compose: `DB_HOST=pgsql`, `DB_PORT=5432`, `DB_DATABASE=laravel`, `DB_USERNAME=sail`, `DB_PASSWORD=password`. These are local demo values only.

3. Build and start the app and PostgreSQL containers:

   ```bash
   ./vendor/bin/sail up -d --build
   ```

4. Create the Laravel app key, database tables, and made-up demo accounts/data; then build the browser assets:

   ```bash
   ./vendor/bin/sail artisan key:generate
   ./vendor/bin/sail artisan migrate --seed
   ./vendor/bin/sail npm install
   ./vendor/bin/sail npm run build
   ```

5. Open `http://localhost` in Windows or Ubuntu. Sign in with one of the local-only demo accounts in `README.md`.
6. When done, stop the containers:

   ```bash
   ./vendor/bin/sail down
   ```

## 5. How to test the main workflow locally

1. Sign in as the demo teacher and open appointments.
2. Create or view a request for the demo student and choose an available time.
3. Sign in as the demo admin and check the request, change its status, and open the demo case and follow-up.
4. Sign in as the demo student and confirm the student view only shows that student's appointment information.
5. For API checks, see the endpoint list in `README.md`; use only the local demo accounts and local database.

## 6. Prepare a Render test deploy

The repo now has a `render.yaml` Blueprint. A **Blueprint** is a setup file telling Render to create the app service and PostgreSQL database with the right connection. It uses the repo's Dockerfile, `/up` health check, PHP 8.5, and PostgreSQL 18. Automatic online demo seeding is disabled.

Before deploying:

1. Make sure Docker can build the image locally if possible:

   ```bash
   docker build -t guidancesched-render-test .
   ```

2. Commit the deployment files and push them to a GitHub branch your Render account can access. Never commit `.env`, a real password, or an `APP_KEY`.
3. In Render, choose **New → Blueprint**, connect the GitHub repo, and select the branch containing `render.yaml`.
4. Render will show the app and database it plans to create. Select the free test plans if offered, review the resources, then apply/create them.
5. When prompted for `APP_KEY`, paste a fresh Laravel key. Generate one on a PHP 8.4+ machine from the project folder with:

   ```bash
   php artisan key:generate --show
   ```

   If you cannot run Artisan locally, generate a key using the deployed container shell after it is built, then enter it in Render's environment settings and redeploy. Keep the key private and do not change it after real encrypted data or user sessions exist.
6. The Blueprint connects `APP_URL` and `ASSET_URL` to the HTTPS URL Render gives the service.
7. Wait for the deploy to show **Live** and the `/up` health check to pass. Open the service URL and confirm the login page loads.
8. Create a test admin account in Render's service shell with `php artisan tinker`, using a new password only for the test deployment. For example, in Tinker run:

   ```php
   \App\Models\User::create([
       'name' => 'Test Admin',
       'email' => 'your-email@example.com',
       'role' => 'admin',
       'password' => \Illuminate\Support\Facades\Hash::make('your-new-test-password'),
   ]);
   ```

   Do not use the public demo password. The deployed container intentionally does not seed demo users.
9. Sign in and try one appointment flow using fictional data. Check the Render logs if the page or database connection fails.

Free Render web services can go idle and take time to wake up. Free Render PostgreSQL is temporary and expires after 30 days, so this is only a short-lived test environment. Use fictional data only. Review the current [Render Laravel and Docker guide](https://render.com/docs/deploy-php-laravel-docker), [Blueprint guide](https://render.com/docs/blueprint-spec), and [Render PostgreSQL guide](https://render.com/docs/postgresql-creating-connecting) while setting it up.

## 7. What the deployment files do

- `Dockerfile` builds the CSS/JavaScript files, installs Laravel's production PHP packages, configures Apache on Render's web port, runs database migrations, and starts the website.
- `render.yaml` tells Render to make the test web service and PostgreSQL database, link them with a private database address, and check `/up` to see whether the site is running.
- `.env` is for local settings only. On Render, set private values in the Render dashboard rather than adding them to Git.
- `database/seeders/DatabaseSeeder.php` is for fictional local demo records; run `migrate --seed` locally, not as part of the public Render startup.

## 8. What “test deploy” means here

A test deploy means putting a temporary copy online and checking that it starts, connects to PostgreSQL, shows the login page, and completes a basic appointment workflow with made-up records. It does not prove that the website is ready for real student information or that it can handle production traffic.

@push('scripts')
    <script>
        document.getElementById('toggle-password')?.addEventListener('click', function () {
            const password = document.getElementById(this.getAttribute('aria-controls'));
            const isVisible = password.type === 'text';
            password.type = isVisible ? 'password' : 'text';
            this.textContent = isVisible ? 'Show' : 'Hide';
            this.setAttribute('aria-pressed', String(!isVisible));
            this.setAttribute('aria-label', isVisible ? 'Show password' : 'Hide password');
        });
    </script>
@endpush

        </section>

<script>
    const mobileMenuToggle = document.querySelector('.mobile-menu-toggle');
    const appNavigation = document.querySelector('.app-sidebar');
    mobileMenuToggle?.addEventListener('click', () => {
        const isOpen = appNavigation.classList.toggle('is-open');
        mobileMenuToggle.setAttribute('aria-expanded', String(isOpen));
        mobileMenuToggle.setAttribute('aria-label', isOpen ? 'Close navigation menu' : 'Open navigation menu');
        mobileMenuToggle.textContent = isOpen ? '×' : '☰';
    });
    document.addEventListener('keydown', (event) => {
        if (event.key === 'Escape' && appNavigation?.classList.contains('is-open')) {
            appNavigation.classList.remove('is-open');
            mobileMenuToggle?.setAttribute('aria-expanded', 'false');
            mobileMenuToggle?.setAttribute('aria-label', 'Open navigation menu');
            if (mobileMenuToggle) mobileMenuToggle.textContent = '☰';
            mobileMenuToggle?.focus();
        }
    });

    window.setupAvailableSlotPicker = function (dateSelect, timeSelect, endpoint, selectedTime = '', requireSelection = false, appointmentId = null) {
        let requestNumber = 0;
        const refresh = async () => {
            const currentRequest = ++requestNumber;
            const date = dateSelect.value;
            const previousSelection = selectedTime || timeSelect.value;
            timeSelect.replaceChildren(new Option('Choose a time', ''));
            timeSelect.disabled = true;
            timeSelect.required = requireSelection || Boolean(date);
            if (!date) return;

            const params = new URLSearchParams({ date });
            if (appointmentId) params.set('appointment_id', appointmentId);
            try {
                const response = await fetch(`${endpoint}?${params}`, { headers: { Accept: 'application/json' }, cache: 'no-store' });
                if (currentRequest !== requestNumber || dateSelect.value !== date) return;
                if (!response.ok) throw new Error(`HTTP ${response.status}`);
                const { times } = await response.json();
                if (currentRequest !== requestNumber || dateSelect.value !== date) return;
                if (times.length === 0) timeSelect.options[0].text = 'No open times for this date';
                times.forEach((time) => timeSelect.add(new Option(time.label, time.value, false, time.value === previousSelection)));
                timeSelect.disabled = false;
                selectedTime = '';
            } catch (error) {
                if (currentRequest !== requestNumber) return;
                timeSelect.replaceChildren(new Option(`Unable to load available times${error.message ? ` (${error.message})` : ''}`, ''));
            }
        };
        dateSelect.addEventListener('change', () => {
            selectedTime = '';
            refresh();
        });
        refresh();
    };
</script>

@push('scripts')
    <script>
        document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded', () => {
            const role = document.getElementById('role');
            const profile = document.querySelector('[data-student-profile]');
            const syncStudentFields = () => {
                const isStudent = role.value === 'student';
                profile.hidden = !isStudent;
                profile.querySelectorAll('[data-student-required]').forEach((field) => field.required = isStudent);
            };
            role.addEventListener('change', syncStudentFields);
            syncStudentFields();
        });
    </script>
@endpush

    <section class="card">
        <div class="card-header">
            <div>
                <h2 class="card-title">All accounts</h2>
                <p class="card-subtitle">{{ $users->total() }} registered users</p>
            </div>
            <a class="button" href="{{ route('users.create') }}">+ Add user</a>
        </div>

        <form class="filter-bar" method="GET" action="{{ route('users.index') }}">
            <input name="search" type="search" value="{{ request('search') }}" placeholder="Search by name or email" aria-label="Search users">
            <select name="role" aria-label="Filter by role">
                <option value="">All roles</option>
                @foreach (['admin' => 'Administrator / Guidance', 'teacher' => 'Teacher', 'student' => 'Student'] as $value => $label)
                    <option value="{{ $value }}" @selected(request('role') === $value)>{{ $label }}</option>
                @endforeach
            </select>
            <button class="button button-secondary" type="submit">Filter</button>
            @if (request()->hasAny(['search', 'role']))<a class="button button-secondary" href="{{ route('users.index') }}">Clear</a>@endif
        </form>

        <div class="table-scroll">
            <table>
                <thead><tr><th>Name</th><th>Email</th><th>Role</th><th>Joined</th><th>Actions</th></tr></thead>
                <tbody>
                @forelse ($users as $user)
                    <tr>
                        <td class="primary-text">{{ $user->name }}</td>
                        <td>{{ $user->email }}</td>
                        <td><span class="badge">{{ $user->role === 'admin' ? 'Administrator / Guidance' : ucfirst($user->role) }}</span></td>
                        <td>{{ $user->created_at?->format('M j, Y') ?? '—' }}</td>
                        <td>
                            <div class="actions">
                                <a class="button button-secondary button-small" href="{{ route('users.edit', $user) }}">Edit</a>
                                @if ($user->id !== auth()->id())
                                    <form class="inline-form" method="POST" action="{{ route('users.destroy', $user) }}" onsubmit="return confirm('Delete this user account?')">
                                        @csrf @method('DELETE')
                                        <button class="button button-danger button-small" type="submit">Delete</button>
                                    </form>
                                @endif
                            </div>
                        </td>
                    </tr>
                @empty
                    <tr><td colspan="5"><div class="empty-state"><div class="empty-icon">♙</div><strong>No user accounts found</strong><div>Try changing your search or role filter.</div></div></td></tr>
                @endforelse
                </tbody>
            </table>
        </div>
        @if ($users->hasPages())<div class="pagination">{{ $users->withQueryString()->links() }}</div>@endif
    </section>
@endsection

FILE: routes/api.php
SIZE: 1462 bytes

<?php

use App\Http\Controllers\Api\GuidanceApiController;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Route;

// These routes send JSON data to another program instead of returning Blade web pages.
// HTTP Basic authentication keeps this small prototype API stateless.
// The API controller still performs role and record-ownership checks after authentication succeeds.
Route::middleware('auth.basic')->group(function () {
    // Appointment API: list, create, view, update, and cancel requests.
    Route::get('/appointments', [GuidanceApiController::class, 'appointments']);
    Route::post('/appointments', [GuidanceApiController::class, 'createAppointment']);
    Route::get('/appointments/{appointment}', [GuidanceApiController::class, 'showAppointment']);
    Route::patch('/appointments/{appointment}', [GuidanceApiController::class, 'updateAppointment']);
    Route::patch('/appointments/{appointment}/cancel', [GuidanceApiController::class, 'cancelAppointment']);

    // Case API: administrators can view cases and record follow-up work.
    Route::get('/cases', [GuidanceApiController::class, 'cases']);
    Route::get('/cases/{case}', [GuidanceApiController::class, 'showCase']);
    Route::patch('/cases/{case}/status', [GuidanceApiController::class, 'updateCaseStatus']);
    Route::get('/cases/{case}/follow-ups', [GuidanceApiController::class, 'followUps']);
    Route::post('/cases/{case}/follow-ups', [GuidanceApiController::class, 'createFollowUp']);
});

FILE: routes/web.php
SIZE: 3638 bytes

<?php

use App\Http\Controllers\AppointmentController;
use App\Http\Controllers\CaseRecordController;
use App\Http\Controllers\DashboardController;
use App\Http\Controllers\FollowUpController;
use Illuminate\Support\Facades\Route;
use App\Http\Controllers\UserController;

Route::get('/', function () {
    return redirect()->route('dashboard');
});

// Every browser page below requires the user to sign in first.
// This middleware does not decide what each role may do; controllers enforce role and record ownership.
Route::middleware('auth')->group(function () {
    // The dashboard shows counts based on the signed-in user's role.
    Route::get('/dashboard', [DashboardController::class, 'index'])->name('dashboard');

    // Consultation pages: view requests, check free times, and manage schedules.
    Route::get('/appointments', [AppointmentController::class, 'index'])
        ->name('appointments.index');
    Route::get('/appointments/available-times', [AppointmentController::class, 'availableTimes'])
        ->name('appointments.available-times');

    Route::post('/appointments', [AppointmentController::class, 'store'])
        ->name('appointments.store');
    Route::get('/appointments/{appointment}/edit', [AppointmentController::class, 'edit'])->name('appointments.edit');
    Route::put('/appointments/{appointment}', [AppointmentController::class, 'update'])->name('appointments.update');
    Route::patch('/appointments/{appointment}/cancel', [AppointmentController::class, 'cancel'])->name('appointments.cancel');

    // Student profile pages are available to teachers and administrators.
    Route::get('/students', [UserController::class, 'students'])->name('students.index');
    Route::get('/students/{user}/details', [UserController::class, 'editStudentDetails'])->name('students.details.edit');
    Route::put('/students/{user}/details', [UserController::class, 'updateStudentDetails'])->name('students.details.update');

    // Account management pages. The controller allows administrators only.
    Route::get('/users', [UserController::class, 'index'])->name('users.index');
    Route::get('/users/create', [UserController::class, 'create'])->name('users.create');
    Route::post('/users', [UserController::class, 'store'])->name('users.store');
    Route::get('/users/{user}/edit', [UserController::class, 'edit'])->name('users.edit');
    Route::put('/users/{user}', [UserController::class, 'update'])->name('users.update');
    Route::delete('/users/{user}', [UserController::class, 'destroy'])->name('users.destroy');
    Route::patch('/users/{user}/password', [UserController::class, 'resetPassword'])->name('users.reset-password');

    // Confidential case and follow-up pages. The controller allows administrators only.
    Route::prefix('cases')->name('cases.')->group(function () {
        Route::get('/', [CaseRecordController::class, 'index'])
            ->name('index');

        Route::get('/create', [CaseRecordController::class, 'create'])
            ->name('create');

        Route::post('/', [CaseRecordController::class, 'store'])
            ->name('store');

        Route::get('/{case}/edit', [CaseRecordController::class, 'edit'])->name('edit');
        Route::put('/{case}', [CaseRecordController::class, 'update'])->name('update');
        Route::post('/{case}/follow-ups', [FollowUpController::class, 'store'])->name('follow-ups.store');
        Route::patch('/{case}/follow-ups/{followUp}', [FollowUpController::class, 'update'])->name('follow-ups.update');

        Route::get('/{case}', [CaseRecordController::class, 'show'])
            ->name('show');
    });
});

FILE: tests/Feature/GuidanceWorkflowTest.php

<?php

namespace Tests\Feature;

use App\Models\Appointment;
use App\Models\CaseRecord;
use App\Models\User;
use Illuminate\Foundation\Testing\RefreshDatabase;
use Tests\TestCase;

class GuidanceWorkflowTest extends TestCase
{
    use RefreshDatabase;

    public function test_student_sees_only_their_own_appointments(): void
    {
        $student = User::factory()->create(['role' => 'student']);
        $otherStudent = User::factory()->create(['role' => 'student']);
        Appointment::create(['student_id' => $student->id, 'title' => 'My request', 'scheduled_at' => now()->addDays(2), 'status' => 'pending']);
        Appointment::create(['student_id' => $otherStudent->id, 'title' => 'Private request', 'scheduled_at' => now()->addDays(2), 'status' => 'pending']);

        $this->actingAs($student)->get(route('appointments.index'))
            ->assertOk()
            ->assertSee('My request')
            ->assertDontSee('Private request');
    }

    public function test_student_cannot_open_staff_case_records(): void
    {
        $student = User::factory()->create(['role' => 'student']);

        $this->actingAs($student)->get(route('cases.index'))->assertForbidden();
    }

    public function test_valid_consultation_request_is_saved_for_signed_in_user(): void
    {
        $teacher = User::factory()->create(['role' => 'teacher']);

        $this->actingAs($teacher)->post(route('appointments.store'), [
            'title' => 'Planning meeting',
            'reason' => 'Synthetic test reason',
            'scheduled_at' => now()->addDays(2)->format('Y-m-d H:i:s'),
        ])->assertRedirect(route('appointments.index'));

        $this->assertDatabaseHas('appointments', [
            'student_id' => $teacher->id,
            'title' => 'Planning meeting',
            'status' => 'pending',
        ]);
    }

    public function test_user_cannot_cancel_another_students_appointment(): void
    {
        $student = User::factory()->create(['role' => 'student']);
        $otherStudent = User::factory()->create(['role' => 'student']);
        $appointment = Appointment::create(['student_id' => $otherStudent->id, 'title' => 'Other request', 'scheduled_at' => now()->addDays(2), 'status' => 'pending']);

        $this->actingAs($student)
            ->patch(route('appointments.cancel', $appointment))
            ->assertForbidden();
    }

    public function test_admin_can_record_a_follow_up_for_a_case(): void
    {
        $admin = User::factory()->create(['role' => 'admin']);
        $student = User::factory()->create(['role' => 'student']);
        $case = CaseRecord::create([
            'student_id' => $student->id,
            'created_by' => $admin->id,
            'case_number' => 'CASE-TEST-001',
            'title' => 'Synthetic case',
            'category' => 'academic',
            'description' => 'Synthetic test content',
            'status' => 'open',
        ]);

        $this->actingAs($admin)->post(route('cases.follow-ups.store', $case), [
            'follow_up_date' => today()->toDateString(),
            'notes' => 'Synthetic follow-up note',
            'action_taken' => 'Study plan discussed',
        ])->assertRedirect(route('cases.show', $case));

        $this->assertDatabaseHas('follow_ups', [
            'case_record_id' => $case->id,
            'created_by' => $admin->id,
            'notes' => 'Synthetic follow-up note',
        ]);
    }

    public function test_api_basic_auth_returns_only_the_signed_in_students_requests(): void
    {
        $student = User::factory()->create(['role' => 'student', 'password' => 'demo-password']);
        $otherStudent = User::factory()->create(['role' => 'student']);
        Appointment::create(['student_id' => $student->id, 'title' => 'API own request', 'scheduled_at' => now()->addDays(2), 'status' => 'pending', 'notes' => 'Private staff note']);
        Appointment::create(['student_id' => $otherStudent->id, 'title' => 'API private request', 'scheduled_at' => now()->addDays(2), 'status' => 'pending']);

        $this->withBasicAuth($student->email, 'demo-password')
            ->getJson('/api/appointments')
            ->assertOk()
            ->assertJsonFragment(['title' => 'API own request'])
            ->assertJsonMissing(['title' => 'API private request'])
            ->assertJsonMissingPath('data.0.notes');
    }

    public function test_student_cannot_read_confidential_cases_through_api(): void
    {
        $student = User::factory()->create(['role' => 'student', 'password' => 'demo-password']);

        $this->withBasicAuth($student->email, 'demo-password')
            ->getJson('/api/cases')
            ->assertForbidden();
    }

    public function test_documented_api_workflows_for_appointments_cases_and_follow_ups(): void
    {
        $student = User::factory()->create(['role' => 'student', 'password' => 'demo-password']);
        $admin = User::factory()->create(['role' => 'admin', 'password' => 'demo-password']);

        $createdAppointment = $this->withBasicAuth($student->email, 'demo-password')
            ->postJson('/api/appointments', [
                'title' => 'API consultation',
                'scheduled_at' => now()->addDays(3)->format('Y-m-d H:i:s'),
                'reason' => 'Synthetic reason',
            ])
            ->assertCreated()
            ->assertJsonPath('data.status', 'pending')
            ->json('data.id');

        $this->withBasicAuth($student->email, 'demo-password')
            ->getJson("/api/appointments/{$createdAppointment}")
            ->assertOk()
            ->assertJsonPath('data.title', 'API consultation');

        $this->withBasicAuth($student->email, 'demo-password')
            ->patchJson("/api/appointments/{$createdAppointment}", ['title' => 'Updated consultation'])
            ->assertOk()
            ->assertJsonPath('data.title', 'Updated consultation');

        $this->withBasicAuth($student->email, 'demo-password')
            ->patchJson("/api/appointments/{$createdAppointment}/cancel")
            ->assertOk()
            ->assertJsonPath('data.status', 'cancelled');

        $case = CaseRecord::create([
            'student_id' => $student->id,
            'created_by' => $admin->id,
            'case_number' => 'CASE-API-001',
            'title' => 'Synthetic API case',
            'category' => 'academic',
            'description' => 'Synthetic confidential data',
            'status' => 'open',
        ]);

        $this->withBasicAuth($admin->email, 'demo-password')
            ->getJson('/api/cases')
            ->assertOk()
            ->assertJsonFragment(['case_number' => 'CASE-API-001']);

        $this->withBasicAuth($admin->email, 'demo-password')
            ->getJson("/api/cases/{$case->id}")
            ->assertOk()
            ->assertJsonPath('data.description', 'Synthetic confidential data');

        $this->withBasicAuth($admin->email, 'demo-password')
            ->patchJson("/api/cases/{$case->id}/status", ['status' => 'in_progress'])
            ->assertOk()
            ->assertJsonPath('data.status', 'in_progress');

        $this->withBasicAuth($admin->email, 'demo-password')
            ->getJson("/api/cases/{$case->id}/follow-ups")
            ->assertOk()
            ->assertJsonPath('data', []);

        $this->withBasicAuth($admin->email, 'demo-password')
            ->postJson("/api/cases/{$case->id}/follow-ups", [
                'follow_up_date' => today()->toDateString(),
                'notes' => 'Synthetic API follow-up',
                'action_taken' => 'Checked in',
            ])
            ->assertCreated()
            ->assertJsonPath('data.notes', 'Synthetic API follow-up');
    }
}
